=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are playing a game.
There are N enemies lined up in a row, and the i-th enemy from the front has a health of H_i.
You will repeat the following action until the healths of all enemies become 0 or less, using a variable T initialized to 0.

- Increase T by 1. Then, attack the frontmost enemy with health 1 or more. If T is a multiple of 3, the enemy's health decreases by 3; otherwise, it decreases by 1.

Find the value of T when the healths of all enemies become 0 or less.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
H_1 H_2 \ldots H_N

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 2\times 10^5
- 1 \leq H_i \leq 10^9
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3
6 2 2

Sample Output 1

8

The actions are performed as follows:

- T becomes 1. Attack the 1st enemy, and its health becomes 6-1=5.
- T becomes 2. Attack the 1st enemy, and its health becomes 5-1=4.
- T becomes 3. Attack the 1st enemy, and its health becomes 4-3=1.
- T becomes 4. Attack the 1st enemy, and its health becomes 1-1=0.
- T becomes 5. Attack the 2nd enemy, and its health becomes 2-1=1.
- T becomes 6. Attack the 2nd enemy, and its health becomes 1-3=-2.
- T becomes 7. Attack the 3rd enemy, and its health becomes 2-1=1.
- T becomes 8. Attack the 3rd enemy, and its health becomes 1-1=0.

Sample Input 2

9
1 12 123 1234 12345 123456 1234567 12345678 123456789

Sample Output 2

82304529

Sample Input 3

5
1000000000 1000000000 1000000000 1000000000 1000000000

Sample Output 3

3000000000

Beware of integer overflow.


Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

def kill_time(prev_t, h):
    # prev_t is time when previous enemy died (0 initially)
    # current enemy first attacked at prev_t+1
    # total damage before current enemy = P(prev_t)
    base = prev_t + 2*(prev_t//3)
    target = base + h
    q, r = divmod(target, 5)
    if r <= 2:
        return 3*q + r
    else:
        return 3*(q+1)

import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data: return
    N = data[0]
    H = data[1:]
    t = 0
    for h in H:
        base = t + 2 * (t // 3)
        target = base + h
        q, r = divmod(target, 5)
        if r <= 2:
            t = 3 * q + r
        else:
            t = 3 * (q + 1)
    print(t)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    if not data:
        return

    it = iter(map(int, data))
    n = next(it)

    t = 0
    for _ in range(n):
        h = next(it)

        # Total damage dealt up to time t
        base = t + 2 * (t // 3)

        # Need P(T) >= base + h
        target = base + h
        q, r = divmod(target, 5)

        if r <= 2:
            t = 3 * q + r
        else:
            t = 3 * (q + 1)

    print(t)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
